# Robotech: Battlecry (Xbox, XDK 4721) - the symbols of default.xbe
#
# Addresses and names only: no game data or code lives in this file. tools/symbols.py reads and writes
# it; tools/apply_names.py puts the names into the disassembly, the recompiler emits them, and
# tools/organize_gen.py lays the C out in files with each function's description.
#
# Format:  ADDRESS  KIND  NAME  [# comment]
#   ADDRESS  the Xbox virtual address, 8 hexadecimal digits
#   KIND     func = a function's entry, data = a variable or table
#   ADDRESS  file  PATH  [# what the stretch holds]
#            the functions from ADDRESS up to the next file line go to src/recomp/gen/PATH.c; a file may
#            take several stretches, and the comment of its first line describes it
#   comment  NAME  text
#            what the function NAME does, in the game's terms and with the C's names
#   ADDRESS  var  NAME  TYPE [STRUCT]
#            a variable: TYPE u8 s8 u16 s16 u32 s32 f32 f64 char, TYPE[N] an array of N, TYPE[] one of
#            unknown length; a STRUCT after a u32 says it points at one. TYPE a structure's name is a
#            record of it in place, STRUCT[N] a table of N records (the C: STRUCT_field(NAME(i)))
#   struct  NAME  SIZE                     a structure of the game (SIZE in hexadecimal)
#   field   STRUCT  OFFSET  NAME  TYPE [STRUCT]
#            one of its fields (OFFSET in hexadecimal); a field nobody has named is not listed
#   type  FAMILY  NUMBER  NAME  [# what it is]
#            the game's object type NUMBER of the family FAMILY (symbols/registries.json) is NAME: its
#            registering function is NAME_Register and its callbacks NAME_Startup, NAME_StateMachine...
#
# A name is real -- it says what the thing is -- or there is none: a type's number, a slot's offset or an
# address is not a name (tools/noise_report.py counts any that reaches the C).
#
# Sections headed "(generated by ...)" are rewritten by tools/symbols.py; every other line is written by
# hand, and a hand line wins over a generated one for the same address.

# ======== names given by hand ========
# The XDK
00157AC0  func  Direct3DCreate8                     # mov eax, 1; ret 4; XbSymbolDatabase does not know it
comment Direct3DCreate8 The XDK's Direct3DCreate8: hands back 1. The game calls it once, before Direct3D_CreateDevice.

0014E6E6  func  xapi_GetTickCount                   # mov eax, [KeTickCount]; mov eax, [eax]; ret
comment xapi_GetTickCount XAPI's GetTickCount: the milliseconds since the console started, the kernel's KeTickCount.

001488B5  func  crt_vsprintf                        # LIBCMT vsprintf: a string FILE (_IOWRT | _IOSTRG), _output, the terminator
comment crt_vsprintf LIBCMT's vsprintf: formats into a buffer from a va_list. Hands back the characters written.

00166B54  var   D3D__DirtyFlags  u32                  # Direct3D's flags of what changed since its last draw
00166B58  var   D3D__TextureState  u32[128]           # Direct3D's record of the texture stages' deferred states, 32 a stage
00166D58  var   D3D__RenderState  u32[166]            # its record of the deferred render states, by D3DRS number
0018EC40  var   D3DDIRTYFROMTEXTURESTATE  u32[22]     # the dirty flags each deferred texture stage state sets
0018EC98  var   D3DDIRTYFROMRENDERSTATE  u32[44]      # the dirty flags each deferred render state sets, from D3DRS 0x5C on
0018ED48  var   D3DSIMPLERENDERSTATEENCODE  u32[92]   # the GPU method each simple render state is pushed with

00013F10  func  xapi_XGetVideoFlags                # beside XGetVideoStandard: ExQueryNonVolatileSetting(8), bits 16 to 22 masked by 0x5F
comment xapi_XGetVideoFlags XAPI's XGetVideoFlags: the video flags of the console's settings (widescreen, 720p, 1080i, 480p, letterbox, PAL 60), less the three high-definition ones unless the console started with the video mode 1 (the kernel's HalBootSMCVideoMode) and a standard other than PAL-I.

# The engine's object system (src/game/engine/objects.c; symbols/classes.json has the classes). An object is a
# handle, (type << 16) | index: its class is vcClassByType[type], its instance the class's instances[index].
001102C0  func  vcObjectSystemInit
00110260  func  vcClassFindFreeInstance               # thiscall: the class in ecx

# Callbacks two classes share, named after the class that has them first (symbols/classes.json leaves a
# shared one unnamed)

0023B4E8  data  vcClassTable                          # the classes, a vcClass (0x70 bytes) per type
002BF1E0  var   vcClassByType  u32[]  vcClass         # each type's class in vcClassTable, 64 types
0023B4A0  data  vcObjectSystemFile                    # a vcFile vcObjectSystemShutdown closes; nothing in the game opens it
0023D0E8  var   vcDataDir  char[260]                  # the folder the objects' files are under: vcObjectSystemInit's argument
0023D1EC  var   vcPlatformNames  char[32]             # "pc", "xbox", "ps2", "gc", 8 bytes each; this build's is "xbox"
0023D210  var   vcReleaseQueueCapacity  s32           # how many handles vcReleaseQueue has room for
0023D214  var   vcReleaseQueueCount  s32              # the handles waiting in it
0023D218  var   vcReleaseQueue  u32                   # the handles released later (vcObjectReleaseLater), a table of u32
0023D21C  var   vcObjectReportMissing  u32            # when set, vcObjectGetShared formats the path of an object it cannot find
002BF2E0  var   vcObjectCacheHandle  u32              # the handle the game last looked up (its own code does it inline)
002BF1C0  var   vcObjectCacheData  u32                # that handle's object; both are cleared when an instance table moves or the handle is released
0023A48C  var   vcGameTime  u32                       # the game's milliseconds since its current state started (0 while a level loads)
001B9A40  data  vcFileMagicVC1                        # the first word of an object's file of the first version, 1A 31 43 56: refused
001B9A44  data  vcFileMagicVC2                        # the first word of an object's file the game reads, 1A 32 43 56
001B9A4C  data  vcFileChunkEnd                        # the word that ends an object's chunks, 1A 21 43 56
001A8AA8  data  vcObjectPathFormat                    # "%s\%s\%s\%s.%s": vcDataDir, the platform, the class, the name, the tag
001A8AB8  data  vcMissingObjectFormat                 # "%s\%s.%s": the class, the name, the tag

# The object system's structures, as its functions read them; a field nobody has seen used is not listed.
struct  vcClassDesc  5C                         # what a class registers: vcRegisterClass copies it into its vcClass
field   vcClassDesc  00  type      u32          # the class's type: its index in vcClassTable and vcClassByType, a handle's high 16 bits
field   vcClassDesc  04  name      char[32]     # the class's name: vcTexture; the folder of its files
field   vcClassDesc  24  file_tag  char[4]      # the extension of its files: txr
field   vcClassDesc  28  version   u32          # the newest version of the object its load callback reads
field   vcClassDesc  2C  chunk_version  u32     # the newest version of a chunk its load_chunk callback reads
field   vcClassDesc  30  flags     u32          # 1: its objects are files of their own, with names unique in the class, which vcObjectCollectChanged lists to be saved apart; 4: vcObjectGetShared clones instead of sharing; 8: no path is reported for a missing object
field   vcClassDesc  34  create    u32          # create(handle): allocates the object, which it hands back
field   vcClassDesc  38  destroy   u32          # destroy(handle, object): frees the object and lets go of its children
field   vcClassDesc  3C  collect   u32          # collect(handle, object, changed, list): collects the children, hands back the changed bits with theirs
field   vcClassDesc  40  load      u32          # load(handle, file, version, object): reads the object, by the version
field   vcClassDesc  44  reload    u32          # reload(handle, file, version, object): reads an object that already exists again
field   vcClassDesc  48  load_chunk  u32        # load_chunk(handle, file, version, object): reads one chunk of the object
field   vcClassDesc  4C  copy      u32          # copy(handle, object): fills a new object from another
field   vcClassDesc  50  in_use    u32          # in_use(handle, object, time): nonzero while the object is still in use at that time
field   vcClassDesc  54  get_size  u32          # get_size(handle, object, what): the object's size in bytes
struct  vcClass  70                             # a class in vcClassTable: its vcClassDesc (whose fields sit at the same offsets), then its instances
field   vcClass  5C  free_index  s32            # where the next instance is taken from
field   vcClass  60  count       s32            # the instances in use
field   vcClass  64  capacity    s32            # the instances the table has room for: vcRegisterClass's second argument, doubled when it fills
field   vcClass  68  peak        s32            # the most instances in use at once
field   vcClass  6C  instances   u32  vcInstance # its table of instances, capacity x 0x4C bytes
struct  vcInstance  4C                          # an object of a class: a handle's low 16 bits index it in the class's instances
field   vcInstance  00  object  u32             # the object's own data, which the class's callbacks work on; 0 for a free instance
field   vcInstance  04  refs    s32             # the references to it; at 0 it is destroyed
field   vcInstance  08  flags   u32             # bit 0: its data changed; bit 1: its name; bit 2: it is new (the three set at creation, cleared once it is read from a file); bit 4: in vcReleaseQueue
field   vcInstance  0C  name    char[64]        # its name, lower case, unique among its class's if the class says so
struct  vcCollectList  8                        # what vcObjectCollectChanged fills: the handles of the objects to save
field   vcCollectList  00  whole   u32          # nonzero: every object goes in, changed or not
field   vcCollectList  04  count   u32          # the handles in it, which follow from offset 8


comment vcObjectSystemInit Starts the object system: no class registered, the folder the objects' files are read from, and the names of the platforms whose folders they may be in.
comment vcClassFindFreeInstance Sets the class's free_index to a free instance: the first from free_index on, else the last before it, else the end of the table.
comment vcGetDataDir The folder the objects' files are under (vcDataDir).
comment vcGetPlatformName The folder of this platform's files under each class's: "xbox".
comment vcRegisterClass Registers an engine class: copies its descriptor (the type, its name, its file tag and the callbacks the object system calls) into vcClassTable, points vcClassByType at it and allocates its table of instances.
comment vcObjectCreate Creates an object of a class: takes a free instance (the table doubles when it is full), marks it changed with one reference and runs the class's create callback. Hands back the new handle.
comment vcObjectAddRef Adds a reference to an object, if the handle is not 0. Hands the handle back.
comment vcClassAddRefAll Adds a reference to every object of a class that has one.
comment vcObjectRelease Takes a reference from an object; at none left, the class's destroy callback frees it and the instance is free again.
comment vcClassDecRefAll Takes a reference from every object of a class that has one, destroying none.
comment vcObjectGetName The object's name.
comment vcObjectMarkChanged Marks the object's data changed.
comment vcObjectClearChanged Marks the object as read from its file: nothing of it changed, nor new.
comment vcObjectFind The object of a class with that name (no name: the first one in use), from the given handle's index on, or among those in use. 0 if there is none.
comment vcClassForEach Calls a function over the objects of a class in use, at most the given number of them (any, if negative), until it hands back 0. Hands back 0 if it stopped so, 1 otherwise.
comment vcObjectClone Makes a new object of the same class as the given one and fills it from it with the class's copy callback.
comment vcObjectGetSize The object's size in bytes, from its class's get_size callback (0 if it has none).
comment vcObjectCollectChanged Collects what has to be saved: an object of a class whose objects are files of their own goes in the list when it or one of its children changed (its class's collect callback goes over the children); the others are saved with whoever refers to them. Hands back whether that one changed with it.
comment vcObjectReleaseLater Puts the object in the release queue while its class's in_use callback says it is still in use; it keeps a reference there.
comment vcObjectReleaseQueued Goes over the release queue: lets go of the objects no longer in use, keeps the others.
comment vcObjectLoadChunk Reads one chunk of an object from a file (the file first, then the handle) through its class's load_chunk callback. Hands back 0 at the end of the chunks, 1 for a chunk read.
comment vcObjectSystemShutdown Ends the object system: the release queue, every class's table of instances and the classes go.
comment vcObjectSetName Names an object (lower case, at most 64 bytes). Refused if its class wants names unique and one of its objects has that name.
comment vcObjectGetShared The object of a class with that name, shared (one more reference) or cloned if the class wants copies. 0 if there is none.
comment vcObjectGet The object of a class with that name: the one loaded already, or read from its file, vcDataDir\xbox\CLASS\NAME.TAG.
comment vcObjectLoad Reads an object from a file: a reference to one read before in it, one the game has by name (shared, or read again), or a new one read through its class's load callback. Hands back its handle with a reference for the caller, 0 if none.
comment vcObjectReload Reads an object from a file into one that exists (the given handle, or the one of that name) through its class's reload callback.
comment vcDefault_ReturnZero What a class gives for a callback that has nothing to do: hands back 0.
comment vcDefault_ReturnOne What a class gives for a callback that only has to succeed: hands back 1.
comment vcDefault_ReturnArg3 What a class with no children gives for its collect callback: hands back the changed bits it was given.
comment vcDefault_CollectFirstChild The collect callback of a class whose object's first word is the handle of its one child: collects that child (vcObjectCollectChanged) and hands back the changed bits it was given with the child's.

comment vcRandomCosmeticSpread A number in (-1, 1] from the cosmetic sequence.
comment vcRandomReset Sets both sequences' seeds and restarts the game's, so its draws repeat; the cosmetic one keeps counting.

# The game's objects (game/objects): the players, entities, weapons, effects, screens... of the game, each of a
# type of a family (GameObjectFamily, GameObjectType; symbols/registries.json has them all, the type lines below
# name them). An object is a handle: its entry's index in its type's pool (bits 0 to 10), its type (11 to 17), its
# family (18 to 22) and a serial (23 to 30), never 0 while it lives. Each object is a state machine: its type's
# answers the messages (GameObjectMessage) it is sent, in its state; and it may have an update run every frame.
0001D430  func  GameObjectEntryFree                   # the handle in eax
0001D4E0  func  GameObjectRegisterFamily
0001D580  func  GameObjectFromHandle
0001D5E0  func  GameObjectChangeState                 # the entry in esi, its object in edi
0001D670  func  GameObjectQueueMessage

0001D980  func  GameObjectClearQueue

0001E180  func  GameObjectDestroy

0001E3E0  func  GameObjectsShutdown
0001E4E0  func  GameObjectUnloadFamily

00059010  func  DoNothing                             # ret
002B5D80  var   GameObjectFamilies  u32[32]  GameObjectFamily  # each family by its number; 0 for none
001E578C  var   GameObjectTime  u32                   # the time the objects are up to, in milliseconds: vcGameTime as GameObjectsUpdate last saw it
001E57B0  var   GameObjectLastHandle  u32             # the handle GameObjectFromHandle found last; 0 for none
001E57B4  var   GameObjectLastObject  u32             # its object
001E5790  var   GameObjectQueues  u32[2]              # the two buffers of messages delivered later: a GameObjectMessage, then its data, each; from calloc
001E5798  var   GameObjectQueueCapacity  u32[2]       # the bytes each buffer has room for
001E57A0  var   GameObjectQueueUsed  u32[2]           # the bytes each holds
001E57A8  var   GameObjectQueueCurrent  u32           # the buffer messages go in now
001AECB0  var   GameObjectQueueOther  u32             # the other one, which GameObjectDeliverQueued goes through
001E57AC  var   GameObjectQueuedCount  s32            # the messages in the current buffer
struct  GameObjectFamily  218                  # a family of object types: set up at start by a function of its own, registered by GameObjectRegisterFamily
field   GameObjectFamily  000  name        u32       # its name, a string: "Entity"
field   GameObjectFamily  004  startup     u32       # startup(): its first use (GameObjectTypeUse); 0 for none
field   GameObjectFamily  008  shutdown    u32       # shutdown(): its last release (GameObjectTypeRelease); 0 for none
field   GameObjectFamily  010  types       u32[127]  GameObjectType  # its types, by number from 0; 0 for none
field   GameObjectFamily  20C  type_count  s32       # how many numbers its types take
field   GameObjectFamily  210  version     s32       # the newest version of its objects in a file that GameObjectLoad reads
field   GameObjectFamily  214  users       s32       # the uses of its types not released yet
struct  GameObjectType  44                     # an object type: what its registering function fills in and stores in its family
field   GameObjectType  00  size           u32   # its object's size in bytes, after the GameObjectEntry each has
field   GameObjectType  04  startup        u32   # startup(): its first use (GameObjectTypeUse): gets what its objects share; 0 for none
field   GameObjectType  08  shutdown       u32   # shutdown(): its last release (GameObjectTypeRelease): lets go of it; 0 for none
field   GameObjectType  0C  state_machine  u32   # state_machine(entry, object, state, message, data, time): answers a message in a state, nonzero if it did; each new object gets it
field   GameObjectType  10  load           u32   # load(file, type, family_version, version): reads one of its objects from a file (GameObjectLoad), hands back its handle, 0 for none
field   GameObjectType  14  all_destroyed  u32   # all_destroyed(): what GameObjectsDestroyAll calls once every object of the type is gone; no type has one
field   GameObjectType  18  collect        u32   # collect(handle, changed): collects what an object refers to that has to be saved (GameObjectCollect)
field   GameObjectType  1C  version        s32   # the newest version of its objects in a file that its load reads
field   GameObjectType  20  flags          u32   # 2: its objects are paused (GameObjectPauseFamily); its registering function sets it to 0 or 1
field   GameObjectType  24  entries        u32   # its pool: an entry (a GameObjectEntry, then the object) per object, from calloc
field   GameObjectType  28  entries_made   s32   # the entries made so far, the next new one's index
field   GameObjectType  2C  capacity       s32   # the entries the pool has room for: doubled when full, 16 at first
field   GameObjectType  30  first_free     s16   # the first entry of its free list; -1 for none
field   GameObjectType  32  first          s16   # the first entry of the ring of its objects alive; -1 for none
field   GameObjectType  34  count          s32   # its objects alive
field   GameObjectType  38  serial         s32   # the serial the last handle got: 8 bits, never 0
field   GameObjectType  3C  users          s32   # its uses not released yet (GameObjectTypeUse)
struct  GameObjectEntry  1C                    # what comes before each object in its type's pool
field   GameObjectEntry  00  handle         u32   # the object's handle; its serial 0 once destroyed
field   GameObjectEntry  04  state_machine  u32   # its type's state machine, which answers its messages
field   GameObjectEntry  08  update         u32   # update(entry, object, time, elapsed, seconds): what GameObjectsUpdate runs for it every frame, the time elapsed in milliseconds and in seconds; 0 for none
field   GameObjectEntry  0C  state          s16   # its state; 0x7FFF for none
field   GameObjectEntry  0E  next_state     s16   # the state it goes to when flag 1 is set; -1 for none
field   GameObjectEntry  10  flags          u32   # 1: a change of state is pending; 2: paused; 4: being collected
field   GameObjectEntry  14  next           s16   # the next entry in its list, the ring of the objects alive or the free list
field   GameObjectEntry  16  previous       s16   # the one before, in the ring
field   GameObjectEntry  18  timer          s32   # the milliseconds left until it gets the timer message; -1 for none
struct  GameObjectMessage  14                  # a message to an object; in the queue, its data follows it
field   GameObjectMessage  00  id         u32   # what it says; the object system's own: 0xFFFF entry made, 0xFFFE entry freed, 0xFFFD state entered, 0xFFFC state left, 0xFFFB timer, 0xFFFA destroyed, 0xFFF9 started
field   GameObjectMessage  04  sender     u32   # who sends it, a handle; 0 for the object system
field   GameObjectMessage  08  receiver   u32   # who it is for
field   GameObjectMessage  0C  time       u32   # when it is delivered: GameObjectTime then
field   GameObjectMessage  10  data_size  u16   # the bytes of its data
field   GameObjectMessage  12  state      s16   # the state the receiver must be in to get it; -1 for any
comment GameObjectEntryFree Puts an object's entry at the head of its type's free list, off the ring of the objects alive.
comment GameObjectRegisterFamily Registers a family of object types under its number (GameObjectFamilies) and empties each of its types' pools.
comment GameObjectFromHandle The object a handle names while it lives (its entry holds the same handle); 0 if none. Keeps the pair in GameObjectLastHandle and GameObjectLastObject, which many callers look at before calling it.
comment GameObjectChangeState While an object has a change of state pending: tells its state it is left (0xFFFC), moves it to the next one with its timer off, and tells that one it is entered (0xFFFD).
comment GameObjectQueueMessage Copies a message and its data to the end of the current queue, which grows by 1 KB when full. Only GameObjectDeliverQueued calls it, for a message not due yet: nothing in the game queues one, so the queues stay empty.
comment GameObjectDeliver Hands a message to an object's state machine in its state and, if that does not answer it, in none (0x7FFF); then does the change of state it asked for, if any. Hands back whether it was answered.
comment GameObjectSendMessage Sends a message to an object now, in any state, with GameObjectTime for its time. Hands back whether it was answered; 0 with no object.
comment GameObjectDeliverQueued Swaps the queues and goes through the one filled: delivers each message that is due to its receiver, if it lives and is in the state the message asks for, and queues again the ones not due.
comment GameObjectClearQueue Empties the current queue.
comment GameObjectsUpdate Brings the objects up to vcGameTime: goes over each type's ring of objects alive, freeing the destroyed ones' entries; for each one not paused, nor its type, counts its timer down and sends it the timer message (0xFFFB) when it runs out, runs its update with the time elapsed (in milliseconds and in seconds) and does its change of state. Then delivers the queued messages.
comment GameObjectsInit Starts the objects: GameObjectTime from vcGameTime, no families.
comment GameObjectTypeUse Takes a use of a type of a family: the family's startup at its first use, the type's at its own.
comment GameObjectTypeRelease Lets go of a use of a type of a family: the family's shutdown at its last, the type's at its own.
comment GameObjectSetFlags Sets flags of an object's entry.
comment GameObjectClearFlags Clears flags of an object's entry.
comment GameObjectPauseFamily Pauses every type of a family, or lets them go on (their flag 2): GameObjectsUpdate leaves their objects as they are.
comment GameObjectLoad Reads an object from a file: none (0); one read before from it, by its index; or a new one read by its type's load, if the file's versions of its family and type are no newer than theirs, the file then moved on to the object's end. Hands back its handle, 0 for none.
comment GameObjectCollect Has an object's type collect what it refers to that has to be saved, unless it is being collected already (its flag 4 meanwhile). Hands back what that hands back; 0 with no object.
comment GameObjectEntryDestroy Sends an object the destroyed message (0xFFFA) with a flag its state machine may clear to stay alive; unless it does, or when forced (then in no state first), its handle's serial is cleared and GameObjectsUpdate frees its entry.
comment GameObjectCreate Creates an object of a type of a family: an entry from the type's free list, or a new one (a use of the type taken, the pool doubled when full); a handle with the type's next serial; the type's state machine, no state, no update, no timer. Given data, sends it the entry made message (0xFFFF) for a new entry and the started one (0xFFF9) with the data. Hands back its handle, 0 if the pool is full and may not grow.
comment GameObjectDestroy Destroys an object, unless its state machine keeps it alive (GameObjectEntryDestroy, not forced).
comment GameObjectsDestroyAll Destroys every object, forced, freeing their entries, and calls each type's all_destroyed after; GameObjectTime and vcGameTime back to 0.
comment GameObjectEntryDestruct Sends an object's entry the freed message (0xFFFE), in no state, and lets go of the use of its type the entry held: the family's and the type's shutdown at their last.
comment GameObjectsShutdown Frees every entry of every type, alive or free (GameObjectEntryDestruct), and every pool, and forgets the families.
comment GameObjectUnloadFamily For each type of a family in use: frees every entry, alive or free, and the pool, and lets go of the type's uses; then of the family's.
comment GameObjectsUnloadAll Unloads every family in use (GameObjectUnloadFamily); GameObjectTime and vcGameTime back to 0.
comment GameObjectCreateInFile Creates an object (GameObjectCreate) and adds it to a file's table of the objects read from it, where the file's later references find it. Hands back its handle.
param   GameObjectFromHandle   arg0  handle
param   GameObjectSetFlags     arg0  handle
param   GameObjectSetFlags     arg1  flags
param   GameObjectClearFlags   arg0  handle
param   GameObjectClearFlags   arg1  flags
param   GameObjectPauseFamily  arg0  family
param   GameObjectPauseFamily  arg1  paused
param   GameObjectDestroy      arg0  handle
comment DoNothing Does nothing: the startup and shutdown of many object types, and the registering function of the onesleft out of the build.

# Callbacks several object types share (tools/symbols.py merge leaves a shared one to a hand name), named after
# what the types that have it are
00019500  func  Entity_Load                           # every Entity type's but the vehicles'
000484B0  func  Veritech_StateMachine                 # every VF-1's: VF1DX to VF1SM
00052BB0  func  Vehicle_StateMachine                  # MechHauler's, MilitaryTransport's, Centipede's, ZenRetrieval's
00052EB0  func  Vehicle_Load
0005B850  func  Missile_StateMachine                  # the missiles', Entity 70 to 77: rdf_ and zen_light_missile, heavy_missile
0006A180  func  Excalibur_StateMachine                # Excalibur's and the three Hiro ones' (hiro_destroid, hiro_disabled)
00071170  func  FemalePowerArmor_StateMachine         # Entity 24's and 35's, both female_powerarmor
00075BC0  func  MalePowerArmor_StateMachine           # MalePowerArmor's and Entity 55's (gorian)
000D3240  func  BooleanTrigger_Load                   # BooleanANDTrigger's and BooleanORTrigger's
000D3330  func  Trigger_Collect                       # every trigger's but three
000385E0  func  Node_Collect                          # 16 of the 26 nodes'
000D7910  func  Item_Load                             # every item's but Item 4's
000EF220  func  ThrustEffect_StateMachine             # the thrusts', Effect 27 to 38
0010B4E0  func  ChunksEffect_StateMachine             # the chunks', Effect 7 to 22
0010C020  func  MuzzleFlashEffect_StateMachine        # the muzzle flashes', Effect 39 to 51
0010C340  func  TrailEffect_StateMachine              # the missiles' contrails (Effect 23 to 25) and MotionTrailEffect's
00028120  func  WeaponCreate
000EF310  func  MissileLauncherCreateMissile          # the launcher's handle in eax, the missile's start data in ecx
comment WeaponCreate Creates a weapon of a type (GameObjectCreate, the Weapon family) with five words of start data from its other arguments. Hands back its handle.
comment MissileLauncherCreateMissile Creates the missile a launcher fires (GameObjectCreate, the Entity family): the missile's type goes with the launcher's weapon type (none for SuperVeritechPackWeapon and SumoPackWeapon, whose launchers are weapons of their own). Hands back its handle.
comment Entity_Load Reads an object of an Entity type from a file (GameObjectLoad), for every Entity type but the vehicles (Vehicle_Load): creates it with the 0x5C bytes of start data it reads (GameObjectCreateInFile), then reads the rest by the version. Hands back its handle, 0 if the rest is not there.
comment Veritech_StateMachine The state machine of every Veritech type, the VF-1s (VF1DX to VF1SM): how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment Vehicle_StateMachine The state machine of the vehicles, MechHauler, MilitaryTransport, Centipede and ZenRetrieval: how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment Vehicle_Load Reads a vehicle (MechHauler, MilitaryTransport, Centipede, ZenRetrieval) from a file (GameObjectLoad). Hands back its handle.
comment Missile_StateMachine The state machine of the missiles (Entity 70 to 77, their assets rdf_light_missile, rdf_heavy_missile, zen_light_missile and zen_heavy_missile): how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment Excalibur_StateMachine The state machine of Excalibur and of the three types with its animations and Hiro's assets (hiro_destroid, hiro_disabled): how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment FemalePowerArmor_StateMachine The state machine of the two female power armour types (female_powerarmor): how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment MalePowerArmor_StateMachine The state machine of MalePowerArmor and of the male power armour with the asset gorian: how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment BooleanTrigger_Load Reads a BooleanANDTrigger or a BooleanORTrigger from a file (GameObjectLoad). Hands back its handle.
comment Trigger_Collect Collects what a trigger refers to that has to be saved (GameObjectCollect): every trigger's collect but EntityProximityTrigger's, SpawnLevelQuotaTrigger's and Trigger 5's.
comment Node_Collect Collects what a node refers to that has to be saved (GameObjectCollect), for 16 of the 26 node types.
comment Item_Load Reads an item from a file (GameObjectLoad), for every item type but Item 4. Hands back its handle.
comment ThrustEffect_StateMachine The state machine of the thrust effects (Effect 27 to 38): how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment ChunksEffect_StateMachine The state machine of the chunk effects (Effect 7 to 22, the chunks and the character damage chunks): how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment MuzzleFlashEffect_StateMachine The state machine of the muzzle flash effects (Effect 39 to 51): how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.
comment TrailEffect_StateMachine The state machine of the trail effects, the missiles' contrails (Effect 23 to 25) and MotionTrailEffect: how their objects answer each message in each state (GameObjectDeliver). Hands back whether one was answered.

comment vcFileFill Learns the file's size, allocates its 64 KB buffer and reads the start of the file into it. Hands back 0 if there is no memory for the buffer.
comment vcFileRead Reads bytes from the file through its buffer, refilling it as the read goes past it (a whole 64 KB part goes straight to the reader). Hands back how many were asked for, or 0 at the end of the file.
comment vcFileCreate Creates a file on vcFileDrive to be written, replacing one of that name. Hands back whether it could.
comment vcFileOpen Opens a file of the game's disc to be read and fills its buffer. Hands back whether it could.
comment vcFileClose Frees the file's buffer and closes it. Hands back 0 if the console would not close it.
comment vcFileSeek Moves the next read to a place in the file: within the buffer, or reading the 64 KB from there.
comment vcFileSkip Moves the next read on by a number of bytes, as vcFileSeek.
comment vcFileTell Where in the file the next read is.
comment vcFileReadIfOpen Reads the given number of bytes, if the file is open. Hands back whether they were there.
comment vcFileReadBytes Reads the given number of bytes. Hands back whether they were there.
comment vcFileReadU16 Reads a 16-bit word. Hands back whether it was there.
comment vcFileReadU32 Reads a 32-bit word. Hands back whether it was there.
comment vcFileReadU16s Reads the given number of 16-bit words. Hands back whether they were there.
comment vcFileReadU32s Reads the given number of 32-bit words. Hands back whether they were there.
comment vcFileExists Whether a file of that name is on vcFileDrive.
comment vcFileAddObject Adds an object read from the file to its table, which doubles when full (32 to start). Hands back its index there.
comment vcFileFreeObjects Frees the file's table of objects.
comment vcFormat Formats a string into the one of the two buffers not written last, which it hands back (empty with no format).
comment vcFormatAppend Formats a string onto the end of the buffer vcFormat wrote last, which it hands back.
comment vcFormatLast The buffer vcFormat wrote last.
comment vcFormatClear Empties the buffer vcFormat wrote last, which it hands back.
comment vcStrLower Turns a string lower case in place.
comment vcRectSet Sets a rectangle's left, top, right and bottom. Hands back 0 with no rectangle.
comment vcRectCopy Copies a rectangle into another. Hands back 0 if either is missing.
comment vcRectInflate Grows a rectangle by the given amounts on each side (left and right, top and bottom). Hands back 0 with no rectangle.
comment vcRectOffset Moves a rectangle by the given amounts. Hands back 0 with no rectangle.
comment vcFileReadRect Reads a rectangle from a file: its left, right, top and bottom, in that order.
comment vcRectPlace Places a rectangle against vcScreenRect: its left from the screen's left (1) or right (2), its top from the screen's top (4) or bottom (8), then centred on that point across (16) or down (32); its size stays.
comment vcScreenRectReset Sets vcScreenRect to vcScreenRectDefault.
comment vcScreenSafeAreaInit Sets the title-safe part of the screen, vcScreenRectDefault and its size, and lays the game's screens out in it (vcScreenRect): always the one of 640 x 480, whatever size and video flags it is given.
comment vcScreenGetRect Sets a rectangle to the whole screen, vcScreenWidth x vcScreenHeight. Hands back 0 with no rectangle.
comment vcScreenSetGamma Sets the gamma ramp, red, green and blue alike: entry i is 255 x (i / 255) to the power 1 / vcScreenGamma, at most 255.
comment vcScreenInit Creates Direct3D and the device: 640 x 480, 32 bits, two back buffers multisampled, a depth buffer with a stencil, shown at every vertical blank. Then sets the gamma, clears and shows the screen twice, and sets the screen's size, its title-safe part and the viewport's rectangle.
comment vcScreenShutdown Releases the device and forgets Direct3D.
comment vcScreenOpen Sets the renderer's default render states and makes its vertex buffers, then clears the screen and shows it. The game hands it the screen's size, the bits of a pixel and a 1, which it does not read.
comment vcScreenSetViewport Sets the viewport to a rectangle, with the whole depth range, and keeps the rectangle in vcViewportRect.
comment vcScreenClear Clears a rectangle of the screen: to a colour, if asked, and its depth to a value (with the stencil, if there is one), if asked.
comment vcScreenClearDepth Clears the depth of a rectangle of the screen to a value, with the stencil if there is one.
comment vcScreenShow Shows the frame drawn: swaps the buffers. The game hands it a 0, which it does not read.
comment vcSceneObjectRegisterType Registers an object type's table of scene object functions (vcSceneObjectTypes), ten words copied.
comment vcSceneObjectGet The vcSceneObject of an object, from its type's get function.
comment vcSceneObjectGetFlags An object's scene flags.
comment vcSceneObjectSetFlags Sets an object's scene flags; if they change, vcSceneObjectFlagsChanged puts it in or takes it out of the quadtrees and passes them to its children.
comment vcSceneObjectChangeFlags Sets some of an object's scene flags and clears others (the cleared win), as vcSceneObjectSetFlags.
comment vcSceneObjectSetUser Sets what the game keeps with an object: the entity it belongs to.
comment vcSceneObjectGetUser What the game keeps with an object: the entity it belongs to.
comment vcSceneObjectGetTransform An object's rotation (three rows) and position in the world.
comment vcSceneObjectGetBounds An object's bounding box in its own frame: its least and greatest corners.
comment vcSceneObjectInit Starts a scene object: no rotation, at the origin, an empty bounding box, its handle and flags, no parent and no slot.
comment vcSceneObjectCopy Makes an object's scene object a copy of another vcSceneObject, but for its own handle, no user, no places in the spatial tables and none in the tree (no parent, child or next sibling, no slot); the previous sibling is left as the other's.
comment vcSceneObjectRemove Takes a scene object out of the quadtrees and off its parent, and lets go of its children.
comment vcSceneObjectAttach Attaches an object to a parent at a slot (-1: nowhere in particular), first taking it off the one it had; with no parent it is only taken off.
comment vcSceneObjectReleaseChildren Takes every child off a scene object and lets go of the reference it held to each.
comment vcSceneObjectUnlink Takes a scene object off its parent's children and lets go of the reference the parent held.
comment vcSceneObjectLink Makes a scene object its parent's first child, placed in its frame, and takes a reference to it.
comment vcSceneObjectPlaceInParent Works out a scene object's rotation and position in its parent's frame (at a slot of the parent, or at the parent's origin), unless its flags keep them.
comment vcSceneObjectFlagsChanged What a change of an object's scene flags does: puts it in or takes it out of each quadtree as flags 4 and 0x10 say, then passes the change to its children.
comment vcSceneObjectInheritFlags Gives a scene object its parent's flags, but for the ones it keeps (0x1F000, and 0x10, 4 or 2 when its 0x1000, 0x2000 or 0x10000 says so), and does what the change does.
comment vcSceneObjectPassFlags Has each child of a scene object inherit its flags.
comment vcSceneObjectReadTransform A scene object's rotation (three rows) and position in the world, copied out.
comment vcSceneObjectInsertBounds Puts a scene object in vcSceneBoundsTree, or moves its entry there, as the sphere around its bounds placed by the given rotation and position: the box's centre transformed, half its diagonal for the radius (-1 for an empty box); a radius under a ten-thousandth leaves it out.
comment vcSceneObjectInsertHitBounds The same for its hit bounds and vcSceneHitTree; with a radius under a ten-thousandth it moves an entry it has to a centre it did not work out (whatever its stack held) before letting go of it.
comment vcSceneObjectEnterBoundsTree What else a scene object entering vcSceneBoundsTree does: unless its flag 2, hands the vcLight module its position, its bounds and the two lists two of its type's functions give.
comment vcSceneObjectLeaveBoundsTree What else a scene object leaving vcSceneBoundsTree does: unless its flag 2, lets go of every object in the two lists two of its type's functions give.
comment vcBoxCenter The centre of a box (its least corner, then its greatest), each sum stored as a float before it is halved.
comment vcTransformPoint A point turned by a rotation (three rows) and moved by a position. Hands back the output.
comment vcSceneInit Starts the scene: empties the table of types and makes the two quadtrees, centred on the origin with a size of 16000, 2048 entries each.
comment vcSceneShutdown Frees the scene's two quadtrees.
comment vcQuadtreeCreate Makes a quadtree of the given levels over a square (three words of its centre and one of its size), with room for the given number of entries.
comment vcQuadtreeDestroy Frees a quadtree.
comment vcQuadtreeInsert Puts an object in a quadtree as a sphere (its centre and radius) and hands back its entry; 0 for a radius of a ten-thousandth or less.
comment vcQuadtreeMove Moves a quadtree's entry to a new sphere.
comment vcQuadtreeRemove Takes an entry out of a quadtree and frees it.
comment vcTextInit Reads the default font, defaultfont.tga, into the first free place of vcFonts and makes it the font text is drawn with; the first call only, until as many vcTextShutdown.
comment vcTextShutdown Lets go of every font once the last vcTextInit is ended.
comment vcTextSetSize Sets the characters' height in pixels (-1: the default, 16). Hands back the height before, as a float.
comment vcTextGetSize The characters' height in pixels, as a float.
comment vcTextSetFont Sets the font text is drawn with, by its index in vcFonts. Hands back the one before.
comment vcTextWidthN The width in pixels of a string's first characters, at most all of them: in the default font each character's own width, in another font the same for all, the height times the scale.
comment vcTextWidth The width in pixels of a string.
comment vcTextSetScale Sets the characters' width as a factor. Hands back the factor before, as a float.
comment vcTextDraw Draws a string at a point, in a colour, with the font, height and scale set.
comment vcTextSetShadowColor Sets the colour of the shadows vcTextDrawAligned draws.
comment vcTextSetShadowOffset Sets how far right of and below its text a shadow is.
comment vcTextDrawAligned Draws a string at a point, centred across on it (1) and down (2), with its shadow behind it (4), as the flags say.
comment vcTextDrawInRect Lays a string out in lines within a rectangle's width, breaking them at the last space or after the character that does not fit (carriage returns are dropped), and draws them from its left or across its centre (flag 1), from its top or down its centre (flag 2), the first line moved right by an indent. Hands back the number of lines.
comment vcFontDrawString Draws a string with a font's texture at a height and a scale, a quad per character, pushed to the GPU 64 at a time; proportional with the default font's widths if told so.
comment vcRenderInit Sets the render states and the first two texture stages' states to the engine's defaults, on the device and in the engine's record of them; then makes the engine's vertex shaders and the shadows' render texture and materials.
comment vcRenderSetState Sets a render state, unless it is already so in vcRenderStates: a simple one is pushed to the GPU, a deferred one recorded in Direct3D's own record for the next draw, a complex one set by its own function, as the XDK's D3DDevice_SetRenderState does.
comment vcRenderSetTextureStageState Sets a texture stage's state, unless it is already so in vcRenderTextureStageStates: the deferred ones recorded in Direct3D's own record for the next draw, the others set by their own functions, as the XDK's D3DDevice_SetTextureStageState does.
comment vcRenderBuffersInit Makes the renderer's vertex buffers: four of 512 KB, four of 384 KB, one of 768 KB, one of 4 MB, and one more.

# The pads: each port's pad read once a frame with XInputGetState (vcPadUpdate, vcPadRead), the sticks and
# buttons as a byte each, 24 of them in the engine's own order (0 to 3 the sticks' axes, 4 and 5 the sticks
# pressed in, 6 to 9 the d-pad, 10 and 12 the triggers, 14 to 17 X A Y B, 18 white, 19 black, 20 START, 21
# BACK; 11, 13, 22 and 23 none of the Xbox's). From the last two reads each control gets a value, and
# whether it was pressed, released or pressed twice in this frame, which is what the game asks.
00111300  func  vcPadInit
00111330  func  vcPadShutdown
00111340  func  vcPadReset
001113A0  func  vcPadClearPresses
00111410  func  vcPadUpdate
00111700  func  vcPadSetDeadZones
001123E0  func  vcPadCloseAll
00112400  func  vcPadIsOpen
00112420  func  vcPadGetHandle
00112430  func  vcPadUpdateDevices
00112530  func  vcPadCalibrate                        # the port in eax, the state in ecx
00112690  func  vcPadRead
param   vcPadReset  arg0  port
param   vcPadClearPresses  arg0  port
param   vcPadSetDeadZones  arg0  stick
param   vcPadSetDeadZones  arg1  button
param   vcPadIsOpen  arg0  port
param   vcPadGetHandle  arg0  port
param   vcPadCalibrate  eax  port
param   vcPadCalibrate  ecx  state
param   vcPadRead  arg0  port
param   vcPadRead  arg1  read
comment vcPadInit Starts the pads: their platform's part, then the eight pads' records and vcPadSeen cleared.
comment vcPadShutdown Closes every port's pad (vcPadCloseAll).
comment vcPadReset Clears a pad's record, and centres the sticks in its last two reads.
comment vcPadClearPresses Clears a port's pad's pressed and pressed-twice flags, so that nothing after it in the frame takes this frame's presses again; the four ports' with a port under 0.
comment vcPadUpdate Once a frame, after vcPadUpdateDevices, for each of the eight pads: the last read becomes the read before, a port's pad is read (vcPadRead), and each control gets from the two reads its value, whether it was pressed or released in this frame, and whether it was pressed twice within vcPadDoublePressTime. A pad's first read is copied to the read before too (vcPadSeen), so what is held then is no press. What fills the four pads after the ports' is not read yet.
comment vcPadSetDeadZones Sets vcPadStickDeadZone and vcPadButtonDeadZone.
comment vcPadCloseAll Closes each port's open pad (XInputClose).
comment vcPadIsOpen Whether a port has a pad open.
comment vcPadGetHandle A port's pad's handle, 0 with none open.
comment vcPadUpdateDevices Asks which pads were taken out and put in since the last time (XGetDeviceChanges): closes the ones taken out and opens the ones put in (XInputOpen), their sticks' calibration started again.
comment vcPadCalibrate Widens a port's stick ranges to the furthest each stick has been seen to go, and zeroes a stick's axis within its dead zone: 10000 of 32767 until the stick has reached both ends of both axes, 4096 after.
comment vcPadRead Reads a port's pad with XInputGetState into the engine's read of it: the sticks as bytes, the digital buttons as 0 or 255, an analog button under 30 as 0. Nothing for a port with no pad open.
001B9A54  var    vcPadStickDeadZone  s32              # how far from its centre, 128, a stick's byte has to be for vcPadUpdate not to take it as centred
001B9A58  var    vcPadButtonDeadZone  s32             # a button's byte under this is 0 for vcPadUpdate
001B9A5C  var    vcPadDoublePressTime  u32            # two presses of a control less than this apart, in vcGameTime's units, are a press twice
0023D224  var    vcPadSeen  u32[8]                    # 1 once a pad's first read was copied to its read before
0023D2AC  var    vcPadHandles  u32[4]                 # each port's pad's handle, from XInputOpen; 0 with none open
0023D2BC  var    vcPadOpened  u32                     # 1 when vcPadUpdateDevices opened a pad the last time it ran
002BE0C0  var    vcPadCalibrations  vcPadCalibration[4]# each port's sticks' ranges
struct   vcPadCalibration  1C                         # how far a port's sticks have been seen to go (vcPadCalibrate)
field    vcPadCalibration  04  left_full    u32       # 1 once the left stick has reached both ends of both axes
field    vcPadCalibration  08  left_x_min   s16       # the lowest x the left stick has been read at; 32767 when the pad is opened
field    vcPadCalibration  0A  left_x_max   s16       # the highest; -32768 when the pad is opened
field    vcPadCalibration  0C  left_y_min   s16
field    vcPadCalibration  0E  left_y_max   s16
field    vcPadCalibration  10  right_full   u32       # the same for the right stick
field    vcPadCalibration  14  right_x_min  s16
field    vcPadCalibration  16  right_x_max  s16
field    vcPadCalibration  18  right_y_min  s16
field    vcPadCalibration  1A  right_y_max  s16
002BE140  var    vcPads  vcPad[8]                     # each port's pad as the game reads it, and four more vcPadUpdate goes through without a port
struct   vcPad  210                                   # a pad as the game reads it: what vcPadUpdate makes of each control, and the last two reads
field    vcPad  000  left_x                     f32   # the left stick's x: its value: a stick's axis from -1 to 1 (0 within vcPadStickDeadZone of its centre), a button from 0 to 1 (0 under vcPadButtonDeadZone)

# The vehicles' parameters: each vehicle type's file keeps 62 constants (floats and counts) in .rdata after its
# model's and its shadow's names, and its registering function stores the address of each in the type's record.
# After the four vehicles' own lie the same four again, byte for byte, which no code and no word of the image
# points at (the vehicles' shared file's own copies).
struct  VehicleParameters  F8                         # a vehicle type's 62 constants, floats and counts; none named yet
00191E04  var   ZenRetrievalParameters           VehicleParameters
00191F3C  var   CentipedeParameters              VehicleParameters
00192074  var   MilitaryTransportParameters      VehicleParameters
001921AC  var   MechHaulerParameters             VehicleParameters
001922D8  var   MechHaulerParametersCopy         VehicleParameters   # nothing reads it
00192404  var   MilitaryTransportParametersCopy  VehicleParameters   # nothing reads it
00192530  var   CentipedeParametersCopy          VehicleParameters   # nothing reads it
00192654  var   ZenRetrievalParametersCopy       VehicleParameters   # nothing reads it

# The cheats: a word of bits the game menu's codes set. With L and R held, left, up, down, A, right, B,
# START open the code keyboard; its text is set against twelve codes, each answered with a text that says
# what it gives (IDS_CHEAT_P1 to P12 of main.pak's string table), and each bit read where it acts:
#   WEWILLWIN    2             "unlocked all missions": every level open and counted finished (LevelIsOpen,
#                              LevelCountsAsFinished, LevelNext); the main menu sets LevelsAllOpen with it
#   WHERESMAX    4, 0x2000     "all veritechs and awards": every veritech's requirement met (4,
#                              VeritechRequirementMet), every award at its top rank, 3 (0x2000, AwardRank)
#   SUPERMECH    1             "invincibility": no damage to an entity a player controls (EntityTakeDamage)
#   SPACEFOLD    8             "faster gunpod ammo refresh": the gun ammo full at every update (EntityRecharge)
#   MARSBASE     0x10          "faster missile refresh": the missiles back three times as fast (EntityRecharge)
#   MIRIYA       8, 0x10       "faster weapon refresh": both
#   MISSMACROSS  0x80          "all veritech designs": every paint design open (PaintDesignIsOpen)
#   BACKSTABBER  0x400         "one shot kills": the guns' shots deal 100000 (GunHit, SniperHit)
#   SNIPER       0x800         "one shot sniper kills": the sniper's shots deal 100000 (SniperHit)
#   MULTIMAYHEM  0x100         "unlocked all multiplayer missions": every versus arena open (LevelIsOpen)
#   DEATHGRIP    0x4000        "press the fire button to instantly kill targets": X kills the target locked
#                              on (Player_Update)
#   CLEAR                      all off
# Only an entity a player controls has them (its +0x18C a handle of the Player family); invincibility and
# DEATHGRIP only with one player. The main menu keeps them aside and clears them for the training
# (CheatsKeep, CheatsClear; CheatsRestore when it is left), and for a versus match keeps only 0x100, 4 and
# 0x2000, and 0x80; a saved game of a version before 6 clears them (sub_0002EFF0). 0x20, 0x40, 0x200, 0x1000
# and the bits from 0x8000 on no code sets or reads.
0002F350  func  CheatIsOn
0002F370  func  CheatSet
0002F390  func  CheatsClear
0002F3A0  func  CheatsKeep
0002F3B0  func  CheatsRestore
000346B0  func  MainMenuCodeSequence
00034B10  func  MainMenuEnterCode
param   CheatIsOn  arg0  mask
param   CheatSet   arg0  mask
001EA5EC  var   Cheats  u32                           # the cheats on, a bit each (the codes' bits above): kept in the saved game
001EA614  var   CheatsKept  u32                       # Cheats as CheatsKeep found them
001EAB40  var   MainMenuCodeStep  u32                 # how many presses of the code sequence have come in order
001EAB3C  var   MainMenuCodeAnswer  u32               # what the game answers the last code with, a text's number; 0 for no code
comment CheatIsOn Whether every bit of a mask is on in Cheats.
comment CheatSet Turns a mask's bits on in Cheats.
comment CheatsClear Turns every cheat off.
comment CheatsKeep Copies Cheats to CheatsKept.
comment CheatsRestore Copies CheatsKept back to Cheats.
comment MainMenuCodeSequence Follows the code sequence while L and R are held on any pad: a press of the one expected next moves MainMenuCodeStep on, any other or two at once starts it again, and the seventh hands back 1.
comment MainMenuEnterCode Sets the text entered on the code keyboard against the codes: each one turns its cheats on (CLEAR all off) and leaves the number of its answer in MainMenuCodeAnswer; a code taken plays one of C1_MONO.wav to C3_MONO.wav, picked by vcRandomCosmetic.

# What the cheats act on. An entity's stores and its damage: the damage message (0xF001) carries its flags,
# who dealt it and the amount; a kill of the cheats' is an amount of 100000.
0001A700  func  EntityTakeDamage
00018C10  func  EntityRecharge
000409A0  func  Player_Update
000D0C90  func  GunHit
000D10C0  func  SniperHit
param   EntityTakeDamage  arg0  handle
param   EntityTakeDamage  arg1  entity
param   EntityTakeDamage  arg2  damage
param   EntityRecharge    eax   handle
param   Player_Update     arg0  entry
param   Player_Update     arg1  player
param   Player_Update     arg2  time
param   GunHit            arg5  weapon
param   SniperHit         arg5  weapon
comment EntityTakeDamage An entity's answer to the damage message (0xF001; the message's third word the amount, its second who dealt it): takes the amount off its +0x17C while that is above 0 and the rest off its hit points (+0x174). On an entity a player controls, with one player, the amount is 0 under SUPERMECH's invincibility (Cheats' 1) or while the count of Stats' tally 1 is above 0, and in the play mode 2 (sub_000A1710) it is scaled by Difficulty, 0.7 on 0 and 1.1 on 2. Then the effects of its record of damage by how much of its hit points is left.
comment EntityRecharge Brings an entity's stores back up over an update, each by a rate of its EntitySpec times 0.033 up to its most: with one player its hit points (+0x174, hit_points_refill, 0 for every type); its gun ammo (+0xA8, gun_ammo_refill up to gun_ammo) unless its flag 4, or its flags 8 and 2, are on, full at once on an entity a player controls under SPACEFOLD's cheat (Cheats' 8); its missiles (+0xB0, missiles_refill up to missiles) unless its flag 8 is on, three times as fast on an entity a player controls under MARSBASE's (0x10); and its +0x178 by the words at +0x1C and +0x18 of its EntitySpec.
comment Player_Update A Player's update, every frame (its GameObjectEntry's update, set by sub_00040B60): while its entity's flag 0x2000 is on, a sound of the set at 0x1EB398 every 165 ms; with two players, once its +0x3B8's time has passed, its entity's model's scene flag 0x10 off; with one player and DEATHGRIP's cheat (Cheats' 0x4000), once its entity's first target locked on (the entity's +0x1A8) has stayed the same for 250 ms, X on its pad (unless ControllerItemsDisabled has it off) sends that target a damage of 100000. With its entity gone, its +0x3A0 becomes sub_0003F980.
comment GunHit What a shot of a gun (Weapon 0, 1, 23, 24 and 34: sub_000D16B0 sets it on the shot's scene object) does to what it hits: sends it the damage (0xF001) of the weapon's WeaponSpec, 100000 under BACKSTABBER's cheat (Cheats' 0x400) when an entity a player controls fired it; then the hit's effects.
comment SniperHit What a shot of Weapon 2 does to what it hits, the one SNIPER's cheat is read for (its text: one shot sniper kills): sends it the damage (0xF001), the weapon's +0x2C (a fixed one past twice its WeaponSpec's damage with two players and the flag at 0x1BC6B4; times the factor at 0x1A5BE4 on Entity 41 and 55), 100000 under BACKSTABBER's (0x400) or SNIPER's (0x800) when an entity a player controls fired it; then the hit's effects.

# What the player earns: the veritechs to choose (five models, each plain, armored and super, and the
# female power armour in a versus match), their paint designs and the awards (the medals' screens).
struct  VeritechChoice  10                            # a veritech the player can choose, in VeritechChoices
field   VeritechChoice  00  name_string    u32        # its name's number in the string table: "VT-1D", "Armored VF-1A", "Super YF-1R"...
field   VeritechChoice  04  requires       s32        # what opens it (VeritechRequirementMet): -1 nothing
field   VeritechChoice  08  requires_also  s32        # a second; -4 for the armored ones, a level for the super ones, -1 none
field   VeritechChoice  0C  model          u32        # 0 VT-1D, 1 VF-1A, 2 VF-1J, 3 VF-1S, 4 YF-1R, 5 the female power armour
001B1400  var   VeritechChoices  VeritechChoice[16]   # the five models plain, armored and super, then the female power armour
001B1500  var   PaintDesignNames  u32[11]             # each paint design's name, its number in the string table: Trainer, Generic, Jack, Izzy, Rick, Max, Miriya, Patriot, Stealth, Wolf, Skull One
001EA574  var   AwardRanks  s16[19]                   # each award's rank, 0 not won up to 3: in the saved game
001EA59C  var   PaintDesignsEarned  u32[11]           # whether each paint design is earned: in the saved game, which earns the first three (sub_0002EFF0)
00025560  func  LevelRequirementsMet
00025620  func  LevelNext
000251F0  func  VeritechRequirementMet
00049A60  func  VeritechChoiceIsOpen
0002F1C0  func  AwardRank
0002F180  func  PaintDesignEarn
0002F1A0  func  PaintDesignIsEarned
00049B70  func  PaintDesignIsOpen
param   LevelRequirementsMet    arg0  level
param   LevelNext               arg0  level
param   VeritechRequirementMet  arg0  requirement
param   VeritechChoiceIsOpen    eax   choice
param   AwardRank               arg0  award
param   PaintDesignEarn         arg0  design
param   PaintDesignIsEarned     arg0  design
param   PaintDesignIsOpen       esi   design
comment LevelRequirementsMet Whether what a level requires is done: never for one not shown on its own; one with its flag 8 once every other level of its chapter shown on its own is finished; any other once the levels it requires are finished.
comment LevelNext The level the game goes on to after one: a story's level, with WEWILLWIN's cheat (Cheats' 2) the next shown on its own (0 past the last), otherwise the next whose requirements are met and that is not finished, after it and then from the first; the training's 50 to 52 and 52 to 54; a versus arena itself. -1 for none.
comment VeritechRequirementMet Whether a requirement of a veritech to choose (VeritechChoices) is met: -1 none; -2 that the level being played is not the training's (chapter 5); -3, -4 and -5 that award 5, 9 and 15 is won (AwardRank above 0); a level's number that it is finished. Every one under WHERESMAX's cheat (Cheats' 4).
comment VeritechChoiceIsOpen Whether a veritech of VeritechChoices can be chosen: its requirements met (VeritechRequirementMet), the armored ones (whose second is -4) only in a versus arena (a level past 58); the female power armour, the 16th, once award 16 is won and in a versus arena.
comment AwardRank An award's rank in AwardRanks, 0 for one not won up to 3, and 0 for a number out of 0 to 18; 3 for every one under WHERESMAX's cheat (Cheats' 0x2000).
comment PaintDesignEarn Marks a paint design earned (PaintDesignsEarned).
comment PaintDesignIsEarned Whether a paint design is earned (PaintDesignsEarned); 0 for a number out of 0 to 10.
comment PaintDesignIsOpen Whether a paint design can be chosen: in the training (chapter 5) only the first, Trainer; otherwise every one under MISSMACROSS's cheat (Cheats' 0x80), or one earned.

# The game's texts: not in the XBE but in main.pak's vcStringManager object "stringtable", 2,049 of them,
# each asked for by its number (tools/string_table.py lists them: 0x796 IDS_CHEAT_P9...).
001E62DC  var   StringTable  u32                      # the vcStringManager object "stringtable" the game's texts come from; 0 until StringTableInit

000248B0  func  StringTableGet
00111CF0  func  vcStringManagerGetText
param   vcStringManagerGetText  arg0  manager
param   vcStringManagerGetText  arg1  number
comment StringTableInit Once: gets the vcStringManager object "stringtable" (vcObjectGet, class 0x12) into StringTable, or makes one of that name, and hands it to sub_00111BC0. Hands back 0 if it has none.
comment StringTableRelease Lets go of StringTable (vcObjectRelease).
comment StringTableGet The game's string table, StringTable: what vcStringManagerGetText is handed with a text's number.
comment vcStringManagerGetText A text of a vcStringManager by its number: its record's offset (each record 0x14 bytes, the name in 16 and the offset) from where its texts start. 0 for a number past its count or a record without a text.

# The levels: a table of them in place (Levels), each a mission's part, a training or a versus arena,
# with the chapter it is in and the levels that must be finished before it opens; the menus ask these.
001AECB8  var   Levels  Level[76]                     # the 75 levels, and after them one whose flags are -1, which ends them

struct  Level  54                                     # a level in Levels
field   Level  00  flags          u32                 # 1 finished; 4 not shown on its own; 8 opens once the rest of its chapter is finished; 0x80000 a versus arena
field   Level  04  requires       s16                 # a level that must be finished before it opens; -1 none
field   Level  06  requires_also  s16                 # a second one; -1 none
field   Level  08  file           char[32]            # the name of its level's file, which the game loads as NAME_f (m101_f.lvl)
field   Level  28  title_string   u32                 # its title's number in the string table (vcStringManager), on its mission screen
field   Level  50  chapter        u32                 # its chapter: 0 to 4 the story's five, then the other modes' (7 the versus arenas)

param   LevelSetFinished       arg1  finished
param   LevelsSetAllOpen       arg0  open
param   LevelGetChapter        arg0  level
param   LevelCountsAsFinished  arg0  level
comment LevelCount The number of levels in Levels: the entries before the one whose flags are -1.
comment LevelGet A level's entry in Levels, by its number.
comment LevelSetCurrent Sets the level being played (LevelCurrent). Hands back the one before.
comment LevelGetCurrent The level being played (LevelCurrent).
comment LevelIsOpen Whether a level can be chosen: never one not shown on its own; every other with the code that unlocks every mission (Cheats' 2) or while LevelsAllOpen is set; a versus arena with its flag 0x20 or MULTIMAYHEM's cheat (0x100); one with its flag 8 once every other level of its chapter shown on its own is finished; any other once the levels it requires are finished.
comment LevelIsFinished Whether a level is finished (its flag 1).
comment LevelSetFinished Marks a level finished or not.
comment LevelsSetAllOpen Sets LevelsAllOpen.
comment LevelGetChapter A level's chapter.
comment LevelCountsAsFinished Whether a level is finished, or every one is with the code that unlocks every mission (Cheats' 2).

# What the types get by name at their first use and keep at a fixed place until their last release (each
# X_Startup stores it, X_Shutdown lets go of it): a texture, a material, a motion, a particle system or a
# scene the object system finds by its class and name (vcObjectGet: classes 1, 2, 9, 11 and 8); an object
# of a model, made new from the vcMultiSub of that name (sub_00121DF0: class 10, into a vcMultiSubObject,
# class 12); a sound made of the vcSnd of that name (sub_0010E920: class 15, into a vcSound, class 29); the
# first object of a scene (sub_00133CF0); and the number of a model's node, by the node's name
# (sub_0011EA70: -1 for none). Named by the type, the name asked for and what it is; the type alone where
# it keeps one of the kind; a table where the type walks them as one. Not here: the first word of something
# larger not typed yet, which is whatever the C hands on the address of or has bytes after it that nothing
# else reaches (a screen's texture starts a record of 0x3C bytes its drawing functions take, sub_00126A10,
# sub_00126CE0 and sub_00126D00; a veritech's model and an effect's particle system start what the type's
# objects share, which a function of the type hands back the address of; a turret's four motions are the
# record its parameters point at); a name a type stores at several places (the role of each is not read
# yet); the mechs' records of motions (0x55 words each, to be typed whole); and what functions without a
# name get.

# The sprites: an image drawn on the screen is a record of 0x3C bytes, a texture or a part of one with its
# size, where it hangs from the place it is drawn at, a scale and a colour for each corner. Ten functions of
# the engine take one, not named yet: sub_00126D20 (sets it up whole: a texture, a width and a height, the
# whole texture, white), sub_00126D90 (a part of a texture, from a rectangle in texels, flipped across or
# down if asked), sub_00126C90 (its texture), sub_00126CC0 (its offset), sub_00126CE0 (its offset to half
# its size back: centred), sub_00126D00 (its four colours the same), sub_00126C40 (a copy, with a reference
# of its own to the texture), sub_00126C70 (lets go of its texture), sub_00126890 (draws it at a place, if
# any of it is on the screen) and sub_00126A10 (draws it turned by an angle in degrees). Here the sprites
# of the types with a name whose texture, got by name, says which each is; a screen's others (several cut
# from one texture) wait for what each one shows, and so do the two headings with a word after their
# sprite that nothing reaches (MissionFailedScreen's at 0x1E6420, AwardsScreen's at 0x20FED0).
struct  vcSprite  3C                                # an image drawn on the screen
field   vcSprite  00  texture              u32     # its texture's handle (a vcTexture), with a reference of the sprite's own; 0 for none
field   vcSprite  08  width                f32     # its width in pixels, before the scale
field   vcSprite  0C  height               f32     # its height
field   vcSprite  10  offset_x             f32     # where its left edge is from the place it is drawn at: 0, or half its width back when centred
field   vcSprite  14  offset_y             f32     # where its top edge is
field   vcSprite  18  scale                f32     # what its size and its offset are multiplied by: 1 unless set
field   vcSprite  1C  u_left               f32     # the texture's coordinates of its edges: 0 to 1 for the whole texture
field   vcSprite  20  v_top                f32
field   vcSprite  24  u_right              f32
field   vcSprite  28  v_bottom             f32
field   vcSprite  2C  colour_top_left      u32     # the colour of each corner: white unless set
field   vcSprite  30  colour_top_right     u32
field   vcSprite  34  colour_bottom_left   u32
field   vcSprite  38  colour_bottom_right  u32
001E63E4  var   MissionFailedScreenGUIMainMenuSprite                 vcSprite   # its texture "GUI_main_menu.tga", which MissionFailedScreen_Startup gets
001EA700  var   MissionCompleteScreenGUIHeadingsMissioncompleteSprite vcSprite   # its texture "GUI_headings_missioncomplete.tga", which MissionCompleteScreen_Startup gets

# The menus' sounds: a screen's code asks for one by setting a word, and the screen on top plays its own
# sound for it (cursor.wav, Cancel_Back_S.wav, Confirm_S.wav) and clears the word.
001EAB20  var   MenuCursorSoundAsked   u32            # set where a menu's choice moves
001EAB24  var   MenuCancelSoundAsked   u32            # set where a menu is left for the one before
001EAB28  var   MenuConfirmSoundAsked  u32            # set where a menu's choice is taken

# The mission complete screen writes its lines one under another: each sets its rectangle from these and
# moves the next down by its height.
001EA7F4  var   MissionCompleteScreenLineY  f32       # the top of the next line, in pixels
001EA7F8  var   MissionCompleteScreenLineX  f32       # the left of the lines

# What an entity type's objects are made to: a record of 65 words (0x104 bytes) that the type's X_Register
# clears and fills, and that an entity's first word points at. Each word points at one of the type's
# constants in .rdata (a float or a count), but for target_node, motions and death_motions, which point at
# data of the type's own. The shared code reads a constant as the float at the pointer at a word of the
# record at the entity's first word. Read so far, with Ghidra (PENDING.md has how each was told), 21 of the
# 65; a word nobody has read has no field. Here the records of the types with a name: 13 more belong to
# types without one, and the player's veritech's (0x1EB740) is filled by code of its own.
struct  EntitySpec  104                             # an entity type's record of where its constants are
field   EntitySpec  000  hit_points      u32         # points at its hit points: an entity's own count starts from them
field   EntitySpec  004  hit_points_refill  u32      # points at the hit points it gets back an update, times 0.033, with one player (EntityRecharge): 0 for every type
field   EntitySpec  008  gun_ammo        u32         # points at the most gun ammo it holds (the entity's +0xA8): SPACEFOLD's cheat keeps a player's there
field   EntitySpec  00C  gun_ammo_refill  u32        # points at the gun ammo it gets back an update, times 0.033 (EntityRecharge)
field   EntitySpec  010  missiles        u32         # points at the most missiles it holds (the entity's +0xB0)
field   EntitySpec  014  missiles_refill  u32        # points at the missiles it gets back an update, times 0.033 (EntityRecharge); three times that for a player's under MARSBASE's cheat
field   EntitySpec  02C  mass            u32         # points at its mass: a change of its speed times it is the force that takes
field   EntitySpec  030  radius          u32         # points at its radius: the box around its place, how near counts as there, what two entities' are summed to for a collision
field   EntitySpec  034  target_node     u32         # points at the number of its model's node "target", where it is aimed at
field   EntitySpec  038  thrust          u32         # points at the force that speeds it up
field   EntitySpec  03C  brake           u32         # points at the force that stops it
field   EntitySpec  040  thrust_vertical   u32       # points at the force that speeds it up, up or down
field   EntitySpec  044  brake_vertical    u32       # points at the force that stops it, up or down
field   EntitySpec  048  thrust_sideways   u32       # points at the force that speeds it up sideways
field   EntitySpec  04C  brake_sideways    u32       # points at the force that stops it sideways
field   EntitySpec  050  boost           u32         # points at what its top speeds are multiplied by while it boosts: 2, or 3
field   EntitySpec  054  boost_force     u32         # points at what those six forces are multiplied by while it boosts: 5
field   EntitySpec  068  speed_forward   u32         # points at its top speed forward
field   EntitySpec  06C  speed_backward  u32         # points at its top speed backward
field   EntitySpec  070  speed_vertical  u32         # points at its top speed up and down: 0 for what stays on the ground
field   EntitySpec  074  speed_sideways  u32         # points at its top speed sideways
field   EntitySpec  0C0  motions         u32         # points at its record of motions (0x55 words for a mech; 0x30 bytes for a ship, which changes it with its state); 0 for a type with none. The shared code plays the motion at its +0x28 or +0x2C (sub_000417F0)
field   EntitySpec  0D4  camera_lag      u32         # points at how many of its last places the camera's is the mean of: 60 at most
field   EntitySpec  0F0  explode_chance  u32         # points at the chance, 0 to 1, that it blows up when its death's motion ends; it fades otherwise
field   EntitySpec  0F4  death_wait      u32         # points at the milliseconds it waits after its death's motion before either
field   EntitySpec  0F8  fade_time       u32         # points at the milliseconds its remains take to fade
field   EntitySpec  100  death_motions   u32         # points at its record of death motions: two lists of a motion and how many times it plays, one picked at random
001EB9F8  var   ZenRetrievalSpec                         EntitySpec
001EBD50  var   CentipedeSpec                            EntitySpec
001EC0A8  var   MilitaryTransportSpec                    EntitySpec
001EC400  var   MechHaulerSpec                           EntitySpec

# The constants an entity type's record points at (EntitySpec): each type's file keeps them one after
# another in .rdata, a float or a count each, and its X_Register stores each one's address in its record. 35
# types lay them out the same where a field of the record has a name, 0xF4 bytes from the mass; five more
# (ZenCruiserTurret1, ZenReEntryPod, ZenReconShip, ZenShuttle, SeaSergeant) have constants of their own
# among them and are not here, nor the vehicles' (VehicleParameters). A field is the constant EntitySpec's field of that name points at.
struct  EntityConstants  F4                         # an entity type's constants: what its EntitySpec's words point at
field   EntityConstants  000  mass             f32
field   EntityConstants  004  radius           f32
field   EntityConstants  008  hit_points       f32
field   EntityConstants  034  thrust           f32
field   EntityConstants  038  brake            f32
field   EntityConstants  03C  thrust_sideways  f32
field   EntityConstants  040  brake_sideways   f32
field   EntityConstants  044  thrust_vertical  f32
field   EntityConstants  048  brake_vertical   f32
field   EntityConstants  05C  speed_forward    f32
field   EntityConstants  060  speed_backward   f32
field   EntityConstants  064  speed_sideways   f32
field   EntityConstants  068  speed_vertical   f32
field   EntityConstants  07C  boost            f32
field   EntityConstants  080  boost_force      f32
field   EntityConstants  0CC  camera_lag       s32
field   EntityConstants  0E8  explode_chance   f32
field   EntityConstants  0EC  death_wait       s32
field   EntityConstants  0F0  fade_time        s32

# What a weapon type's objects are made to: a record of 7 words that every type of the Weapon family clears
# and fills in its X_Register, at the start of what the type's objects share, and that a weapon's first word
# points at (the type's own function hands its address to the weapon's creation). Each word points at one of
# the type's constants in .rdata. Read with Ghidra, in the family's shared code (PENDING.md has how each was
# told); the second word (0 for the guns, 300 for the heavy cannons, 150 for the launchers) nobody has read.
# The packs fill the first five words, the launchers and the flares all but speed.
struct  WeaponSpec  1C                              # a weapon type's record of where its constants are
field   WeaponSpec  000  cost            u32         # points at what a shot takes from the energy the weapon draws on: no shot with less left
field   WeaponSpec  008  range           u32         # points at how far a shot of it reaches
field   WeaponSpec  00C  reload          u32         # points at the milliseconds from a shot to the next
field   WeaponSpec  010  damage          u32         # points at the damage a hit of it does: what the message to what it hits carries
field   WeaponSpec  014  speed           u32         # points at its shots' speed: the aim leads the target by it, and a shot leaves with it added to its owner's
field   WeaponSpec  018  alert_radius    u32         # points at how near to where it hits the AIs of the other side are told of the hit
